# 13. Predictive AI use cases
> **Track:** OpenShift AI Fundamentals — Corporate/L3
> **Audience:** Senior OpenShift administrators, platform engineers, MLOps engineers, SREs, security engineers, architects, and technical leads
> **Version basis:** Aligned to the Red Hat OpenShift AI Self-Managed 3.5 documentation stream available in July 2026. Always confirm support matrices, API stability, operator channels, and component status for your exact subscription and cluster release.
> **Hands-on project:** Develop and deploy a predictive maintenance or fraud-detection service
---
## Executive definition
Predictive AI learns patterns from historical data to classify, forecast, rank, recommend, detect anomalies, estimate risk, and support decisions with measurable statistical performance.
This module treats the subject as an enterprise platform capability rather than a product-demo feature. The emphasis is architecture, ownership, controls, operability, failure handling, repeatability, and measurable production outcomes.
## Learning outcomes
- Select appropriate predictive patterns.
- Define evaluation metrics.
- Deploy batch and online inference.
- Operate drift and retraining.
- Translate the topic into architecture diagrams, operational procedures, security controls, and acceptance tests.
- Explain trade-offs to application owners, data scientists, auditors, and senior management.
- Diagnose failures using a layer-by-layer and evidence-driven troubleshooting method.
## Scope and assumptions
- A supported Red Hat OpenShift cluster is available or planned.
- The OpenShift AI subscription, operator channel, and support matrix are validated before implementation.
- Identity, DNS, certificates, storage classes, ingress, registry access, and monitoring are treated as shared platform dependencies.
- GPU or alternative accelerator capacity is optional for fundamentals but mandatory for many production training and large-model inference workloads.
- S3-compatible object storage is planned for datasets, pipeline artifacts, model artifacts, and other lifecycle data when required.
- Production changes are delivered through Git, peer review, automated validation, and controlled promotion.
- Examples use placeholders and must not be copied into production without security review.
- Feature status can differ between General Availability, Technology Preview, Developer Preview, and community upstream implementations.
## Topic focus map
### Focus 1: Classification
- Define what **classification** means for Predictive AI use cases.
- Identify the owner, consumer, supporting team, approval authority, and escalation path for classification.
- Document the normal flow, failure flow, recovery flow, evidence requirements, and version constraints for classification.
- Create measurable acceptance criteria for classification, including functionality, security, reliability, performance, and operability.
- Decide what is standardized centrally and what remains project-specific for classification.
- Review how classification changes between development, test, production, edge, hybrid-cloud, and disconnected environments.

## Component relationship map
### Component 1: training data
- **Purpose:** Describe why training data is relevant to Predictive AI use cases and which user or controller consumes it.
- **Inputs:** Identify configuration, credentials, data, artifacts, quotas, network access, and dependencies required by training data.
- **Outputs:** Record APIs, artifacts, logs, metrics, status conditions, and user-visible outcomes produced by training data.
- **Dependencies:** Map OpenShift APIs, storage, DNS, certificates, identity, Operators, and external services used by training data.
- **Security:** Define service accounts, RBAC, secrets, network policy, image trust, audit, and data classification for training data.
- **Reliability:** Define health checks, replica strategy, disruption controls, backup scope, recovery objective, and alerting for training data.
- **Operations:** Establish installation, configuration, upgrade, troubleshooting, support, and decommission procedures for training data.

## Enterprise architecture layers
### Layer 1: Business Outcome And Product Management
1. State the responsibility of the business outcome and product management layer for Predictive AI use cases.
2. Identify authoritative configuration sources for the business outcome and product management layer.
3. List upstream and downstream dependencies of the business outcome and product management layer.
4. Define isolation boundaries and data classification at the business outcome and product management layer.
5. Define SLIs for availability, latency, throughput, quality, saturation, and errors where applicable.
6. Define support ownership and escalation from project team to platform team to vendor.
7. Capture evidence through logs, events, metrics, traces, manifests, approvals, and test results.
8. Design rollback and disaster-recovery behavior for failures at the business outcome and product management layer.

## Reference manifests and command patterns

> These examples are instructional baselines. Validate API versions and component support on your exact OpenShift AI release.

### Project namespace

```yaml
apiVersion: project.openshift.io/v1
kind: Project
metadata:
  name: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/part-of: openshift-ai-fundamentals
    training.redhat.com/topic: "predictive-ai-use-cases"
    environment: lab
    owner: ai-platform-team
```

### ResourceQuota baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ResourceQuota
metadata:
  name: ai-team-quota
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  hard:
    requests.cpu: "8"
    requests.memory: 32Gi
    limits.cpu: "16"
    limits.memory: 64Gi
    requests.storage: 500Gi
    persistentvolumeclaims: "10"
    count/pods: "50"
```

### LimitRange baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: LimitRange
metadata:
  name: ai-team-defaults
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  limits:
    - type: Container
      defaultRequest:
        cpu: "250m"
        memory: 512Mi
      default:
        cpu: "2"
        memory: 4Gi
```

### Default-deny NetworkPolicy

```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: default-deny
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  podSelector: {}
  policyTypes:
    - Ingress
    - Egress
```

### ServiceAccount for controlled automation

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ServiceAccount
metadata:
  name: ai-pipeline-runner
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
automountServiceAccountToken: true
```

### Role for project-level read operations

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: Role
metadata:
  name: ai-project-reader
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
rules:
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["pods", "services", "configmaps", "persistentvolumeclaims"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
  - apiGroups: ["apps"]
    resources: ["deployments", "statefulsets"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
```

### RoleBinding

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: RoleBinding
metadata:
  name: ai-project-readers
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
subjects:
  - kind: Group
    name: ai-data-scientists
    apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
roleRef:
  kind: Role
  name: ai-project-reader
  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
```

### S3-compatible data connection secret pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: lab-object-storage
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
    opendatahub.io/managed: "true"
  annotations:
    openshift.io/display-name: Lab object storage
type: Opaque
stringData:
  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_S3_ENDPOINT: https://s3.example.internal
  AWS_S3_BUCKET: ai-lab
  AWS_DEFAULT_REGION: us-east-1
```

### GitOps application pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: Application
metadata:
  name: predictive-ai-use-cases
  namespace: openshift-gitops
spec:
  project: default
  source:
    repoURL: https://git.example.internal/ai-platform/fundamentals.git
    targetRevision: main
    path: topics/predictive-ai-use-cases
  destination:
    server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
    namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  syncPolicy:
    automated:
      prune: true
      selfHeal: true
    syncOptions:
      - CreateNamespace=true
```

### Baseline validation commands

```bash
oc whoami
oc version
oc get clusterversion
oc get csv -A | grep -i -E 'rhods|opendatahub|nvidia|kueue|serverless|servicemesh'
oc get datasciencecluster -A -o wide
oc get dscinitialization -A -o wide
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-operator
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get routes -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp | tail -100
```
## From-scratch corporate project
### Project goal: Develop and deploy a predictive maintenance or fraud-detection service
### Phase 1: Business framing and success criteria
**Objective:** Apply business framing and success criteria specifically to **Predictive AI use cases**.
1. Define the deliverable and an accountable owner for training data, with explicit consideration of classification.
2. Record assumptions and unresolved questions for feature pipelines, with explicit consideration of regression.

## Detailed hands-on lab sequence
### Lab 1: Collect cluster and operator inventory
**Goal:** Collect cluster and operator inventory for Predictive AI use cases, using training data as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**
1. Perform step 1 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate classification, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
2. Perform step 2 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate regression, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
3. Perform step 3 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate forecasting, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
4. Perform step 4 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate anomaly detection, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
5. Perform step 5 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate recommendation, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
6. Perform step 6 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate feature engineering, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
7. Perform step 7 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate evaluation, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
8. Perform step 8 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate drift, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
**Expected result:**
- The training data state is observable and consistent with the declared configuration.
- Access is restricted to the intended identity and project boundary.
- Logs, events, metrics, and status conditions provide enough evidence for operations.
- Repeating the procedure produces the same result without undocumented manual changes.
**Failure injection:**
- Temporarily introduce a safe lab-only misconfiguration related to regression.
- Record the symptom, first failing layer, root cause, corrective action, and prevention control.
**Cleanup:**
- Restore the declared configuration from Git and verify reconciliation.
- Remove temporary credentials, test artifacts, and elevated access.

## Enterprise control catalogue
### Control 1: Identity And Access
- **Policy statement:** Predictive AI use cases must implement documented identity and access controls for training data.
- **Design requirement:** The control must account for forecasting, tenant boundaries, and version-specific support constraints.
- **Implementation evidence:** Git commit, manifest, configuration export, approval record, and automated test result.
- **Operational evidence:** Metrics, logs, alerts, access review, capacity report, incident record, or restore test as applicable.
- **Failure criterion:** The control fails when unauthorized, untraceable, unsupported, unmonitored, or unrecoverable behavior is possible.
- **Review frequency:** Review after material platform changes and on the organization's scheduled control cycle.

## SRE and operational readiness
### Reliability objective 1: Dashboard Availability
- **Indicator:** Define how dashboard availability is measured for Predictive AI use cases.
- **Objective:** Select a target from business criticality, error budget, dependency reliability, and support capability.
- **Telemetry:** Identify source metrics, log queries, events, synthetic tests, and dashboard panels.
- **Alert:** Alert only when user impact or error-budget risk requires action.
- **Runbook:** Include diagnosis, mitigation, escalation, rollback, and post-recovery validation.
- **Review:** Evaluate trend, saturation, false positives, and improvement actions during service review.

## Troubleshooting playbook
### Scenario 1: Dashboard Is Unavailable
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating Predictive AI use cases and is first observed around model registry.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Taint, toleration, affinity, or node-selector mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get pods -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass regression controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 2: User Cannot See A Project
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating Predictive AI use cases and is first observed around drift analysis.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Artifact metadata mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get csv -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass forecasting controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.

## Production design decisions
### Decision 1: Dedicated Versus Shared Openshift Cluster
- **Question:** Which dedicated versus shared OpenShift cluster pattern best supports Predictive AI use cases?
- **Option A:** Optimizes centralized consistency, reuse, governance, and operational efficiency.
- **Option B:** Optimizes isolation, autonomy, locality, or specialized performance.
- **Selection criteria:** Business criticality, data location, latency, scale, skills, security, availability, cost, and supportability.
- **Evidence:** Benchmark results, threat model, cost model, failure test, and stakeholder approval.
- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.

- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.
## Corporate scenario drills
### Drill 1: Regulated Financial Service
- **Situation:** A regulated financial service must implement Predictive AI use cases with emphasis on classification.
- **Constraint:** feature pipelines has a strict availability, security, scale, or connectivity requirement.
- **Task:** Produce a design, implementation sequence, validation plan, rollback, and operating model.
- **Evidence expected:** Diagram, ADR, manifests, test output, SLO, runbook, risk register, and cost estimate.
- **L3 challenge:** Defend the design against a competing pattern and identify the condition that would change your decision.

### Drill 60: Customer-Facing Generative Assistant
- **Situation:** A customer-facing generative assistant must implement Predictive AI use cases with emphasis on retraining.
- **Constraint:** approval gate has a strict availability, security, scale, or connectivity requirement.
- **Task:** Produce a design, implementation sequence, validation plan, rollback, and operating model.

## Expanded glossary
### Term 1: AI platform
- **Definition:** A managed set of services, controls, and workflows for creating and operating AI solutions.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate AI platform to training data, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 2: MLOps
- **Definition:** Practices that automate and govern the machine-learning lifecycle.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate MLOps to feature pipelines, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 3: GenAIOps
- **Definition:** Operational practices for foundation-model, RAG, prompt, agent, evaluation, and inference lifecycles.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate GenAIOps to workbench, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 4: ModelOps
- **Definition:** Portfolio-wide governance and operations for models, versions, approvals, deployments, and risk.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate ModelOps to AI pipeline, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 5: Workbench
- **Definition:** A project-scoped interactive development environment such as JupyterLab or code-server.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate Workbench to model registry, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 6: Pipeline
- **Definition:** A repeatable workflow of containerized tasks for data and model lifecycle automation.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate Pipeline to model serving, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 7: Model registry
- **Definition:** A governed metadata and artifact catalog for model versions and lifecycle state.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate Model registry to batch scoring, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 8: Model catalog
- **Definition:** A curated discovery source for models that can be evaluated, registered, or deployed.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate Model catalog to online inference, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 9: Model serving
- **Definition:** Runtime services that expose trained models through network APIs.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate Model serving to metrics, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 10: Inference
- **Definition:** Execution of a trained model to produce a prediction or generated output.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate Inference to drift analysis, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 11: RAG
- **Definition:** Retrieval-augmented generation that grounds model responses in retrieved enterprise content.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate RAG to explanations, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 12: Embedding
- **Definition:** A numeric vector representation used for semantic similarity and retrieval.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate Embedding to approval gate, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 13: Vector database
- **Definition:** A system optimized to index and search embedding vectors.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate Vector database to training data, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 14: Foundation model
- **Definition:** A broadly trained model that can be adapted to many downstream tasks.
- **Relevance:** In Predictive AI use cases, relate Foundation model to feature pipelines, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 15: Distributed workload

## Final production-readiness statement
A production implementation of **Predictive AI use cases** is ready only when functionality, security, reliability, performance, supportability, cost, auditability, and recovery have objective evidence. A successful demonstration is not equivalent to an operationally supported enterprise service.
